[34m/*
 * Entry point for this C-cret Codex application.  This program can be used
 * to encode and decode plain text using a variety of different substitution
 * ciphers.
 *
 * This program takes [m[31m2[m[34m or [m[31m3[m[34m command line arguments (in addition to this
 * executable's name).  The first argument is an optional -d, which specifies
 * that the message should be decoded rather than encoded with the given key.
 * The next argument is the key that should be used for encoding or decoding.
 * And the last argument is the message.  This program will the print out
 * the resulting encoded/decoded message to standard out.
 *
 * Examples:
 *   ./ccret A abc                -> encoded: ZYX
 *   ./ccret -d A ZYX             -> decoded: ABC
 *   ./ccret cc [m[31m"hello, world"[m[34m    -> encoded: JGNNQ, YQTNF
 */[m
[32mint[m main([32mint[m argc, [32mchar[m** argv) {[38;9H[34m// [m[30m[103mTODO[m[34m: Step [m[31m2.2[m[34m.[39;9H// Update this main method to initialize the following local variables[40;9H// based on the provided command line arguments. [34m/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either [m[31m0[m[34m: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */[m
[32mvoid[m ccret([32mchar[m* key, [32mint[m pDecode, [32mchar[m* message, [32mchar[m* output) {[65;9H[34m// [m[30m[103mTODO[m[34m: Step [m[31m2.6[m[34m.[66;9H// Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with [m[31m'p'[m[34m.[68;9H// create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones[m[69;9H[32mchar[m substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];[70;9HcreateSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);[71;9H[34m// when decoding a message, invert this map[m[72;9H[38;5;130mif[m(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);[74;9H[34m// encode/decode each char in message, one at a time[m[75;9H[38;5;130mfor[m([32mint[m i=[31m0[m;i<strlen(message)+[31m1[m;i++) {[76;17H[32mchar[m c = message[i];[77;17H[34m// convert char to a uppercase letter index [m[31m0[m[34m-LETTER_COUNT[m[78;17H[32mint[m letterIndex = toupper(c) - [31m'A'[m;[79;17H[38;5;130mif[m(letterIndex >=[31m0[m && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)[80;25H[34m// when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution[m[81;25Hc = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + [31m'A'[m;[82;17Houtput[i] = c;[83;9H}

}

[96m/*
 * Entry point for this C-cret Codex application.  This program can be used
 * to encode and decode plain text using a variety of different substitution
 * ciphers.
 *
 * This program takes [m[95m2[m[96m or [m[95m3[m[96m command line arguments (in addition to this
 * executable's name).  The first argument is an optional -d, which specifies
 * that the message should be decoded rather than encoded with the given key.
 * The next argument is the key that should be used for encoding or decoding.
 * And the last argument is the message.  This program will the print out
 * the resulting encoded/decoded message to standard out.
 *
 * Examples:
 *   ./ccret A abc                -> encoded: ZYX
 *   ./ccret -d A ZYX             -> decoded: ABC
 *   ./ccret cc [m[95m"hello, world"[m[96m    -> encoded: JGNNQ, YQTNF
 */[m
[38;5;121mint[m main([38;5;121mint[m argc, [38;5;121mchar[m** argv) {[38;9H[96m// [m[30m[103mTODO[m[96m: Step [m[95m2.2[m[96m.[39;9H// Update this main method to initialize the following local variables[40;9H// based on the provided command line arguments.  When the number of[41;9H// command line arguments is not correct, display usage message and end[42;9H// the program with error code [m[95m1.[m[44;9H[38;5;121mchar[m* key = [95m"KEY"[m;[45;9H[38;5;121mint[m decode = [95m0[m; [96m// [m[95m0[m[96m: encode message, [m[95m1[m[96m: decode message[m[46;9H[38;5;121mchar[m* message = [95m"abc"[m;[47;9H[38;5;121mchar[m output[[95m4[m]; [96m// fix length to hold copy of message[m[49;9Hccret(key,decode,message,output);[50;9Hprintf([95m"[m[38;5;224m%s[m[95m: [m[38;5;224m%s\n[m[95m"[m,!decode?[95m"encoded"[m:[95m"decoded"[m,output);[52;9H[93mreturn[m [95m0[m;
}

[96m/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either [m[95m0[m[96m: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */[m
[38;5;121mvoid[m ccret([38;5;121mchar[m* key, [38;5;121mint[m pDecode, [38;5;121mchar[m* message, [38;5;121mchar[m* output) {[65;9H[96m// [m[30m[103mTODO[m[96m: Step [m[95m2.6[m[96m.[66;9H// Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with [m[95m'p'[m[96m.[68;9H// create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones[m[69;9H[38;5;121mchar[m substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];[70;9HcreateSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);[71;9H[96m// when decoding a message, invert this map[m[72;9H[93mif[m(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);[74;9H[96m// encode/decode each char in message, one at a time[m[75;9H[93mfor[m([38;5;121mint[m i=[95m0[m;i<strlen(message)+[95m1[m;i++) {[76;17H[38;5;121mchar[m c = message[i];[77;17H[96m// convert char to a uppercase letter index [m[95m0[m[96m-LETTER_COUNT[m[78;17H[38;5;121mint[m letterIndex = toupper(c) - [95m'A'[m;[79;17H[93mif[m(letterIndex >=[95m0[m && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)[80;25H[96m// when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution[m[81;25Hc = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + [95m'A'[m;[82;17Houtput[i] = c;[83;9H}

}

[96m/*

[96m/*
 * Entry point for this C-cret Codex application.  This program can be used
 * to encode and decode plain text using a variety of different substitution
 * ciphers.
 *
 * This program takes [m[95m2[m[96m or [m[95m3[m[96m command line arguments (in addition to this
 * executable's name).  The first argument is an optional -d, which specifies
 * that the message should be decoded rather than encoded with the given key.
 * The next argument is the key that should be used for encoding or decoding.
 * And the last argument is the message.  This program will the print out
 * the resulting encoded/decoded message to standard out.
 *
 * Examples:
 *   ./ccret A abc                -> encoded: ZYX
 *   ./ccret -d A ZYX             -> decoded: ABC
 *   ./ccret cc [m[95m"hello, world"[m[96m    -> encoded: JGNNQ, YQTNF
 */[m
[38;5;121mint[m main([38;5;121mint[m argc, [38;5;121mchar[m** argv) {[38;9H[96m// [m[30m[103mTODO[m[96m: Step [m[95m2.2[m[96m.[39;9H// Update this main method to initialize the following local variables[40;9H// based on the provided command line arguments.  When the number of[41;9H// command line arguments is not correct, display usage message and end[42;9H// the program with error code [m[95m1.[m[44;9H[38;5;121mchar[m* key = [95m"KEY"[m;[45;9H[38;5;121mint[m decode = [95m0[m; [96m// [m[95m0[m[96m: encode message, [m[95m1[m[96m: decode message[m[46;9H[38;5;121mchar[m* message = [95m"abc"[m;[47;9H[38;5;121mchar[m output[[95m4[m]; [96m// fix length to hold copy of message[m[49;9Hccret(key,decode,message,output);[50;9Hprintf([95m"[m[38;5;224m%s[m[95m: [m[38;5;224m%s\n[m[95m"[m,!decode?[95m"encoded"[m:[95m"decoded"[m,output);[52;9H[93mreturn[m [95m0[m;
}

[96m/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either [m[95m0[m[96m: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */[m
[38;5;121mvoid[m ccret([38;5;121mchar[m* key, [38;5;121mint[m pDecode, [38;5;121mchar[m* message, [38;5;121mchar[m* output) {[65;9H[96m// [m[30m[103mTODO[m[96m: Step [m[95m2.6[m[96m.[66;9H// Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with [m[95m'p'[m[96m.[68;9H// create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones[m[69;9H[38;5;121mchar[m substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];[70;9HcreateSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);[71;9H[96m// when decoding a message, invert this map[m[72;9H[93mif[m(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);[74;9H[96m// encode/decode each char in message, one at a time[m[75;9H[93mfor[m([38;5;121mint[m i=[95m0[m;i<strlen(message)+[95m1[m;i++) {[76;17H[38;5;121mchar[m c = message[i];[77;17H[96m// convert char to a uppercase letter index [m[95m0[m[96m-LETTER_COUNT[m[78;17H[38;5;121mint[m letterIndex = toupper(c) - [95m'A'[m;[79;17H[93mif[m(letterIndex >=[95m0[m && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)[80;25H[96m// when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution[m[81;25Hc = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + [95m'A'[m;[82;17Houtput[i] = c;[83;9H}

}

        ccret(key,decode,message,output);
        printf([m[1C%s: %s\n[1C[95m,!decode?[m[1Cencoded[1C[95m:[m[1Cdecoded[1C[95m,output);

/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either 0: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */
void ccret(char* key, int pDecode, char* message, char* output) {

        // TODO: Step 2.6.
        // Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with 'p'.

        // create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones
        char substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];
        createSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);
        // when decoding a message, invert this map
        if(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);

        // encode/decode each char in message, one at a time
        for(int i=0;i<strlen(message)+1;i++) {
                char c = message[i];
                // convert char to a uppercase letter index 0-LETTER_COUNT
                int letterIndex = toupper(c) - 'A';             
                if(letterIndex >=0 && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)
                        // when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution
                        c = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + 'A';
                output[i] = c;
        }
        
}

/*
 * Creates a substitution map that can be used for encoding messages using
 * a variety of different ciphers.  This function makes use of the helper
 * functions: createAtbashMap, createCaesarMap, and createMixedMap.  When
 * the first letter of a key is 'a') it creates the map for an Atbash cipher, 
 * 'c') it creates the map for a Caesar cipher, otherwise) it creates a map[45;24H"[m[95m[46m)[m[97;112H1-25[45;25H[?25h[?25l[95m[46m"[m);
        }

        [38;5;121mchar[m* key = [1C[95mKEY[m[1C;
        [38;5;121mint[m decode = [1C; [96m// [1C: encode message, [1C: decode message[m
        [38;5;121mchar[m* message = [1C[95mabc[m[1C;
        [38;5;121mchar[m output[[1C]; [96m// fix length to hold copy of message[m
                        
        ccret(key,decode,message,output);
        printf([1C[38;5;224m%s[m[95m: [m[38;5;224m%s\n[m[1C,!decode?[1C[95mencoded[m[1C:[1C[95mdecoded[m[1C,output);
        
        [93mreturn[m [1C;
}

[38;5;121mvoid[m ccret([38;5;121mchar[m* key, [38;5;121mint[m pDecode, [38;5;121mchar[m* message, [38;5;121mchar[m* output) {

[96m/*

 * a variety of different ciphers.  This function makes use of the helper

/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either 0: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */
void ccret(char* key, int pDecode, char* message, char* output) {

        // TODO: Step 2.6.
        // Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with 'p'.

        // create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones
        char substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];
        createSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);
        // when decoding a message, invert this map
        if(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);

        // encode/decode each char in message, one at a time
        for(int i=0;i<strlen(message)+1;i++) {
                char c = message[i];
                // convert char to a uppercase letter index 0-LETTER_COUNT
                int letterIndex = toupper(c) - 'A';             
                if(letterIndex >=0 && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)
                        // when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution
                        c = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + 'A';
                output[i] = c;
        }
        
}

/*
 * Creates a substitution map that can be used for encoding messages using
 * a variety of different ciphers.  This function makes use of the helper
 * functions: createAtbashMap, createCaesarMap, and createMixedMap.  When
 * the first letter of a key is 'a') it creates the map for an Atbash cipher, [m[97;112H9-63[50;63H[?25h[?25l[38;5;224mn[m[95m"[m);
                [93mreturn[m [1C;
        }

        ccret(key,decode,message,output);
        printf([1C[38;5;224m%s[m[95m: [m[38;5;224m%s\n[m[1C,!decode?[1C[95mencoded[m[1C:[1C[95mdecoded[m[1C,output);
        
        [93mreturn[m [1C;
}

/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either 0: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */
void ccret(char* key, int pDecode, char* message, char* output) {

        // TODO: Step 2.6.
        // Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with 'p'.

        // create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones
        char substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];
        createSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);
        // when decoding a message, invert this map
        if(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);

        // encode/decode each char in message, one at a time
        for(int i=0;i<strlen(message)+1;i++) {
                char c = message[i];
                // convert char to a uppercase letter index 0-LETTER_COUNT
                int letterIndex = toupper(c) - 'A';             
                if(letterIndex >=0 && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)
                        // when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution
                        c = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + 'A';
                output[i] = c;
        }
        
}

        [38;5;121mint[m decode = [1C; [96m// [1C: encode message, [1C: decode message[m
        [38;5;121mchar[m* message = [1C[95mabc[m[1C;
        [38;5;121mchar[m output[[1C]; [96m// fix length to hold copy of message[m

        printf([1C[38;5;224m%s[m[95m: [m[38;5;224m%s\n[m[1C,!decode?[1C[95mencoded[m[1C:[1C[95mdecoded[m[1C,output);
        
        [93mreturn[m [1C;
}

 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.

/*
 * This function does the heavy lifting for the C-cret Codex application.
 * Arugments:
 * key is a c-string reference to the key used for encoding/decoding
 * pDecode is either 0: encode message with key, otherwise message is decoded
 * message is a c-string to the message being encoded or decoded
 * output references the memory where the encoded/decoded string is written
 */
void ccret(char* key, int pDecode, char* message, char* output) {

        // TODO: Step 2.6.
        // Polyalphabetic cipher implemented when key starts with 'p'.

        // create cipher specific mapping from plain letters to encoded ones
        char substitutionMap[LETTER_COUNT];
        createSubstitutionMap(key,substitutionMap);
        // when decoding a message, invert this map
        if(pDecode) invertMap(substitutionMap);

        // encode/decode each char in message, one at a time
        for(int i=0;i<strlen(message)+1;i++) {
                char c = message[i];
                // convert char to a uppercase letter index 0-LETTER_COUNT
                int letterIndex = toupper(c) - 'A';             
                if(letterIndex >=0 && letterIndex < LETTER_COUNT)
                        // when that index is valid letter, use map for substitution
                        c = substitutionMap[letterIndex] + 'A';
                output[i] = c;
        }
        
}[52;24H"[m[95m[46m)[m[97;112H1-25[52;25H[?25h[?25l[95m[46m"[m)
        }

        [38;5;121mchar[m* key = [1C[95mKEY[m[1C;
        [38;5;121mint[m decode = [1C; [96m// [1C: encode message, [1C: decode message[m
        [38;5;121mchar[m* message = [1C[95mabc[m[1C;
        [38;5;121mchar[m output[[1C]; [96m// fix length to hold copy of message[m
        
        ccret(key,decode,message,output);
        printf([1C[38;5;224m%s[m[95m: [m[38;5;224m%s\n[m[1C,!decode?[1C[95mencoded[m[1C:[1C[95mdecoded[m[1C,output);
        
        [93mreturn[m [1C;
}
